#### ORBW00040 ABI mismatch found and corrected package built (2026-10-16)

The next Oblivion launch was captured after restoring the runtime TCP listener.
Runtime log `build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261016-124918.log` confirms the selected
`oblivion-gog` profile and `cpu=native`. Wine resolved `Oblivion.exe` at
`0x00400000`, loaded `wow64native.dll` from `WINE_PS5_WOW64_CPU`, and then
reported `PW_NATIVE_STAGE process_init ... version=4 expected=3` and the same
mismatch in `thread_init`. Both initialization calls were rejected. Klog
recorded SIGSYS at 12:50:34.797; this followed the demonstrated ABI rejection,
so it is not evidence that PS4 lacks the `sys_exit` syscall or that Oblivion
reached guest code.

The packaging cause is confirmed: the package built earlier contained the
stock 40-KB `wow64native.dll` from
`prospero-win/.deps/wine-host/install/usr/lib/wine`, while its PS4 Unix
companion was the ABI-v3 build (`PW_NATIVE_ABI_VERSION 3`). The matching
134-KB diagnostic PE already existed at
`build/orbw00040-live-profile-wow64native/x86_64-windows/wow64native.dll`
(SHA-256 `bcc51a7853d10c47a1565981889834fe19e0a715fe4ddf3e88a717a07e3cf11d`),
but the package build omitted the supported `WOW64NATIVE_DLL` override. The
retrieved coredump is
`build-ps4-logs/ORBW00040_1792180234.sorbisdmp` (199,577 bytes, SHA-256
`828f5d588d2806ce88fdf80c7d854222c8d915ab8dccb0409295be6e229565ca`). The
plain-text klog and runtime log are more useful than this SCECAF container.

Rebuilt the relocatable package with the matching ABI-v3 PE explicitly
staged. Verification confirms the staged DLL hash matches the 134-KB build
and the PS4 companion is unchanged. Artifact:
`pkgout/orbw00040-native-thread-cpu-time-abi3/`
`IV0000-ORBW00040_00-ORBW000400000000.pkg`, SHA-256
`b38b330b7d6bd9f893e8024b6dd20b0eafc16dd639fe8cf4b7a1049e65209faa`.
Both relocated images verified as ELF `DYN`. Uploaded to
`/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00040_00-ORBW000400000000.pkg` (GoldHEN FTP returned
`226`). The previous SIGSYS did not validate the native backend beyond
exposing the packaging mismatch; see the following hardware rerun.

#### ORBW00040 ABI-v3 hardware rerun (2026-10-16)

The corrected ABI-v3 package was installed and launched. Runtime log
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261016-124918.log` shows the `oblivion-gog` profile
with `cpu=native`, `PW_NATIVE_PROVIDER caps=7`, the custom
`wow64native.dll`, `BTCpuProcessInit status=00000000`, and
`BTCpuThreadInit ... version=3 expected=3`. The per-thread low TCB was
allocated at `0x7fff0000`, `PW_NATIVE_SIGNAL_TCB` was logged, and Wine loaded
`Oblivion.exe` at its required base `0x00400000` as `native`. This confirms the
previous package ABI mismatch is fixed and the native backend is executing the
game image; it does not establish stable game execution.

The user saw the spinning dots and confirmed the run was stalled. At
12:57:04.280 Wine logged
`wgl:internal_context_create Failed to create internal thread context`. The
native profile then stopped changing at `host_calls=1481`,
`guest_calls=1482`, `unix_calls=353`, and `syscall_calls=1128`; meanwhile its
thread CPU clock advanced by about 2 seconds every 2 seconds. At close it had
accumulated about 90 seconds of CPU time, while the process heartbeat continued
and `sink_calls` crept only from 891 to 901. Throughout the stall,
`frames_put=0`, `shown=0`, and `audio=0`. The user closed the title from the PS4
menu at 12:58:37; klog reports a normal `BlockingKill` and task end with
`error=0x0`. No coredump was emitted, so the watcher had no file to retrieve.

This run is not another native-start failure: the v3 process/thread init,
signal TCB setup, and fixed-base game image load all succeeded. It also is not
yet enough to blame the WGL warning: it precedes the no-frame stall, but the
available logs do not connect it causally. The sampled native thread is
consuming roughly one core without crossing back through WoW64; we still need
its guest instruction pointer or an equivalent stack sample to locate the
loop.

Correction: the `wowprospero` SIGPROF profiler is part of the x86 translator
and cannot sample a native `cpu=native` thread. The zero-byte
`/data/prospero-win/pw_wow_profile` trigger was removed after confirming it
could not affect this run. Do not use that trigger as a native profiling test.

#### ORBW00040 native PC sampler package (2026-10-16)

Added a native-only SIGPROF diagnostic. Ntdll installs the handler only when
SIGPROF is still at its default disposition, stores the interrupted RIP/RSP/CS
in atomic fields, and advertises the optional sampler in capability bit `0x20`;
the required native capability mask remains `0x07`. `wow64native` uses its
existing per-thread watcher to signal only threads that accumulated more than
100 ms CPU in the previous interval, then logs `PW_NATIVE_PC`. No guest stack
memory is dereferenced. Source patches are
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0911-ntdll-sample-native-wow64-pc.patch` and
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0038-wow64native-sample-native-pc.patch`.

Built the relocatable ORBW00040 package with the matching ABI-v3 Windows DLL
and the updated `ntdll.prx` / `wow64native.prx`. Both relocated images verified
as ELF `DYN`; the staged Windows DLL hash matches the known custom ABI-v3 DLL.
Artifact:
`pkgout/orbw00040-native-pc-sampler/IV0000-ORBW00040_00-ORBW000400000000.pkg`,
SHA-256 `66df4db1b1dd85b22903b6a56c1621bde780dc0539a29a8bbc11690956c8e4c2`.
Uploaded to `/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00040_00-ORBW000400000000.pkg` (GoldHEN FTP
returned `226`). The package was installed and hardware-tested. It enables
native WoW64, and the sampler reports RIP/RSP/CS successfully. During the
Oblivion loading stall, `frames_put=0` persisted while the native thread
accumulated CPU time close to wall-clock time. Most samples were at
`rip=0x8000027da`, `rsp=0x100000d188`; one was at `rip=0x80000293c`. The saved
libkernel image in `codex_scratch/libkernel-text-full.bin` decodes these as the
syscall instruction for syscall 3 (`read`) and syscall `0x79` (`gettimeofday`)
respectively. This locates the hot samples in libkernel syscall stubs, but does
not identify the caller or the read descriptor. The user stopped the run; the
runtime stream then disconnected normally.

#### ORBW00041 native syscall-argument sampler (2026-10-16)

Extended the SIGPROF snapshot with `RAX/RDI/RSI/RDX/R10`, which are the syscall
number and first four argument registers on the x86-64 FreeBSD syscall ABI.
During the next run, samples repeatedly showed `read(fd=0x20, buffer, 0x40)`;
some snapshots were at the syscall instruction with `RAX=3`, and others at
the return check with `RAX=0x40`, showing the read returned 64 bytes. The
thread stayed at `frames_put=0` and accumulated CPU while the app displayed the
spinning dots. Wine protocol request 92 is `REQ_get_key_value`; it made 71 of
the last 100 logged server requests, and 12,816 request records accumulated by
the time the user closed the app from the PS menu. This strongly associates
the busy read with repeated registry-value requests over Wine's server channel,
but the exact registry key/value has not yet been identified. Klog confirms a
normal app close, not a crash. Built and verified both relocated images as ELF
`DYN`:
`pkgout/orbw00041-native-syscall-sampler/IV0000-ORBW00041_00-ORBW000410000000.pkg`
(SHA-256 `87e7276a143c2ff5b22fa837dd39a455d86d42c646db1c3491c8500a24681e1b`).
The matching ABI-v3 `wow64native.dll` override was retained. Uploaded to
`/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00041_00-ORBW000410000000.pkg`; GoldHEN FTP returned
`226`. The syscall sampler patch hunks were reverse-checked against the applied
Wine and prospero-win trees.

Added bounded logging in Wine's `get_key_value` server handler. It records each

without dumping every request. Source patch:
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0912-server-log-hot-registry-value-query.patch`; reverse
application was checked against the applied Wine tree. Built and verified the
relocatable package with both images as ELF `DYN`:
`pkgout/orbw00042-registry-query-diagnostic/IV0000-ORBW00042_00-ORBW000420000000.pkg`
(SHA-256 `47b7ca32bfe9f36b23f5e188801075b087df64ddf79b0ae8a69291af44e1c498`).
Uploaded to `/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00042_00-ORBW000420000000.pkg`; GoldHEN FTP
returned `226`. Install and launch the same Oblivion profile and inspect
`wineserver: getvalue` lines in the klog for the repeated path/value.

ORBW00042 was installed and launched on 2026-10-16 at 13:25. The bounded
registry trace emitted 63 distinct key/value records; the largest repeat count
was 2, and the trace ended during startup at 13:25:37. It did not identify a
hot registry value. This rules out a repeated `get_key_value` loop as the
cause of the later stall. Runtime logs still show `frames_put=0`; from 13:25:24
through at least 13:26:35, every native PC sample returned to libkernel's
`read(fd=0x20, buffer, 0x40)` stub with the same stack pointer and arguments.
One sample caught the post-syscall state with `RAX=0x40`, confirming a
64-byte read completed. Wine's server request stream also stopped at
13:25:37. The native profile counters remained fixed after 13:25:30 while the
reported thread CPU time continued to rise. This is consistent with the
thread repeatedly reading from fd 32. Source mapping identifies it as Wine's
`reply_fd` pipe, whose `wait_reply()` reads the 64-byte response header; it is
not a game-file descriptor. The exact in-flight request and caller are still
unknown. The runtime connection disconnected at 13:28:18 with no frames
presented; klog shows no corresponding crash marker, so the run's end reason
is undetermined. The runtime and klog listeners remain active.

Source mapping narrows fd 32 further: Wine's ntdll `wait_reply()` reads the
64-byte reply header from its `reply_fd` pipe, matching this syscall's fd and
size. The unanswered question is which server request is in flight when the
thread stops making progress. ORBW00043 enables the existing Wine wait
watchdog (patches `0680` and `0690`) so the next run reports the wineserver
thread's last request and wait objects every two seconds. The first ORBW00043
package was mistakenly built with `scripts/build-app-ps4.sh`, despite the hard
rule above to use the relocatable wrapper for Oblivion. That ordinary eboot
occupied Oblivion's preferred image base. On 2026-10-16, Wine reported
`status c0000018` while creating `Oblivion.exe`, then exited back to the still-
running launcher (`reason=wine-exit`). The PS4 title did not crash, and that
run produced no useful wait-watchdog result. The direct builder's watchdog
switch did not cover the relocatable PRX entrypoint either.

#### ORBW00045 hardware result and ORBW00046 watchdog fix (2026-10-16)

ORBW00045 started `oblivion-gog` with native WoW64. The run stayed alive for
about 4 minutes 15 seconds, then the PS4 logged the normal suspend/kill path;
there was no title crash, console crash, or Wine exit. It never rendered or
played audio (`frames_put=0`, `audio=0`, `faults=0`), and about 300 MiB of
flexible memory remained free. The native sampler repeatedly saw the same
64-byte read from fd `0x20`; the profile counters stopped changing after
about 30 seconds (`host_calls=1481`, `guest_calls=1482`, `syscall_calls=1128`).

The 512 registry begin/end pairs all completed, so the previous suspicion of a
stalled registry handler is unsupported. The server's last printed request was
`req 10` (Wine protocol ordinal 10, `get_process_debug_info`), but the client
was already repeatedly sampled in the reply-pipe read and this request has no
handler begin/end instrumentation; it is a lead only. Crucially, the new
server marker reported `wineserver: wait watchdog env missing`, so this run
did not exercise the wait watchdog.

Cause: the relocated entrypoint set `WINE_PS5_WAIT_WATCHDOG` using the title's
libc environment, but the Wine PRX has a separate environment. Patch
`0039-wine64-pass-watchdog-to-wine-environment.patch` now adds the flag to
`PwWineStartConfig.extra_env`, increasing the bounded environment capacity by
one. The ORBW00046 build completed with both ELF images `DYN`; the PRX includes
the relocatable Wine entrypoint and matching ABI-v3 `wow64native.dll`.
Package:
`pkgout/orbw00046-prx-watchdog-env/IV0000-ORBW00046_00-ORBW000460000000.pkg`
(SHA-256 `253bb8ba2d77f28e9b2bbdbc96f4ad250baa5c3a2fcc704d47ebcca45fe40c26`),
uploaded to `/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00046_00-ORBW000460000000.pkg` (GoldHEN FTP
`226`). Install ORBW00046 and repeat the Oblivion launch; first confirm the
`wait watchdog armed` marker, then inspect its two-second snapshots for the
thread's outstanding wait and async state.

#### ORBW00046 failure and ORBW00047 corrected package (2026-10-16)

ORBW00046 crashed during startup. The klog confirms a real process crash:
SIGSYS at `libkernel.sprx+0x28bc`; `RDI=0xc000000d` is the status Wine was
exiting with. The watchdog environment did reach the Wine PRX and the server
logged `wineserver: wait watchdog armed`, so the environment-passing fix itself
worked. No watchdog snapshot fired before the process exited.

The actual launch blocker was a packaging regression. ORBW00046's staged
`x86_64-windows/wow64native.dll` was the stock 40-KB ABI-v4 host-install copy
(SHA-256 `27f74c33b5512419e19c3a1ee91254264bca6bd2f64dc8c135a9cb49ec35b904`),
while its PS4 Unix companion expects ABI v3. Runtime logged `version=4
expected=3` for both `process_init` and `thread_init`; each was rejected with
`STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` immediately before Wine exited. The build command
omitted the supported `WOW64NATIVE_DLL` override, so the packager silently
selected the wrong default. This is the same packaging trap recorded for
ORBW00040; check the staged DLL hash and size on every future build.

`bcc51a7853d10c47a1565981889834fe19e0a715fe4ddf3e88a717a07e3cf11d`) and kept
the watchdog enabled. Both relocated ELF images verify as `DYN`. Package:
`pkgout/orbw00047-oblivion-abi-watchdog/IV0000-ORBW00047_00-ORBW000470000000.pkg`
(SHA-256
`72bad61a09a4d4ab3b1e05e3d705c48e2d31ab37edd93d727a3c08d9a01867ab`),
uploaded to `/data/pkg/IV0000-ORBW00047_00-ORBW000470000000.pkg` (GoldHEN FTP
`226`). Install and test ORBW00047; first verify `process_init` and
`thread_init` both report ABI v3 accepted, then wait for the two-second
watchdog snapshots.

#### ORBW00048 handler trace and ORBW00049 reply-write trace (2026-10-16)

stages:

- `debug_info begin ... handle=ffffffff`
- `debug_info ... pid=0020 obj=0 reply=00000000 children=1 error=c0000353`
- `debug_info end`

`0xc0000353` is `STATUS_PORT_NOT_SET`, the ordinary no-debugger result. The
handler therefore completes; request 10 alone does not explain the client
stall. The next boundary to measure is whether `send_reply()` writes the
64-byte reply to the pipe.

#### ORBW00050 client-read result and ORBW00051 extended request trace (2026-10-16)

ORBW00050 is still at the spinning dots, with `frames_put=0`, `audio=0`, and
no faults. The key new evidence at 14:47:01.300 is:

```
ntdll: debug_reply_poll n=1 fd=32 poll=1 events=0001 ioctl=-1 bytes=-1 errno=13
ntdll: debug_reply_read done n=1 status=c0000353 size=0
```

The server's matching write of all 64 bytes to fd 33, poll's readable event,
and completed client read prove that request 10's reply crossed the pipe

handles `003c`/`0088`, it issued repeated requests with `flags=2`,
`timeout=0`, `size=0`, and cookie `100000e13c`; the sequence reached 131,072.

The zero-size request has no select-op payload. ORBW00052's first decoder
mistook the following context data for an opcode; `0x8664` is the AMD64
machine value, not a select opcode. Combined with the repeated zero-handle
selects and the source call sites, this points to Wine's `wait_suspend()` path
from the x86-64 signal handler. It does not yet establish why that path repeats
or prove that the native WoW64 backend is the cause. In particular, the flat
sampler counter is from a different sampling point and should not be assumed to
identify thread `0024`.

and `Data\\Meshes\\Sky\\Atmosphere.nif`; Wine reported the corresponding

new failure, distinct from the earlier native-mode zero-timeout wait loop. The
translator A/B got past startup and reached gameplay, but is not stable.

At 15:30:43, the Wine trace reports `STATUS_ACCESS_VIOLATION` in
`wineserver.so+0x998c2`, with address `0x101ff9fd22`. The exact build's
`wineserver.shared.elf` resolves that PC to the optional `wait_watchdog()` in
`server/thread.c:1158`: it directly reads the machine field at `cpu + 2`. The
registers show `RAX=0x101ff9fd20`, so the invalid WoW64 CPU pointer plus two
matches the reported fault address exactly. That diagnostic read is the first
fault and the immediate cause of this new crash; it does not establish a game
or translator bug.

thread without Wine data`. Its later crash context says address `0x1c`,

fault already has an exact source and address match, treat this later context
as the exception-reporting failure obscuring the watchdog fault, consistent
with the foreign-thread failure documented for patches 0612/0749. The missing
DirectShow filter warnings just precede the watchdog firing, but are not
evidence that they caused it.

#### ORBW00053 native-loop register trace (2026-10-16)

The ORBW00053 run with patch 0921 remained alive in the native startup stall
for over ten minutes. It passed 262,144 empty, zero-timeout `REQ_select`
requests (last checkpoint 16:21:13) while `frames_put=0`, `audio=0`, and
`faults=0`. Every guest sample remained at `EIP=7bf2e450`, `ESP=0021fff0`,
with the same stack words. In the loaded 32-bit `ntdll.dll` mapping at
`7bf20000`, offset `0xe450` is `RtlUserThreadStart`; the matching package PE
has the same entry instructions. Wine's i386 implementation copies the
thread-entry target from EAX and argument from EBX before jumping to
`call_thread_func`, so EAX/EBX and the other integer registers are the next
useful facts. The process remained alive at 16:22 with no rendered frame.

validation passed. The Wine build rebuilt and linked the modified server, but

reported missing optional PE outputs; the dedicated PS4 PRX build succeeded,
including the instrumented `wineserver.prx`. The standalone support PRXs

#### ORBW00053 wait-suspend call-site trace (2026-10-17)

The user installed the 0925 SIGUSR1-handler diagnostic package and launched
the native `oblivion-gog` profile. It reproduced the startup stall. Runtime
reported `frames_put=0`, `audio=0`, `faults=0`, and 47 mapped images. Native
profile counters stopped at `host_calls=1481`, `guest_calls=1482`, and
`syscall_calls=1128`. Klog recorded repeated zero-timeout `REQ_select` calls;
at checkpoints through 32,768, each result was `STATUS_TIMEOUT` (`0x102`) with
`suspend=0`, `proc_suspend=0`, `state=0`, `waiting=0`, and a valid context.
There were no `ntdll usr1-handler` records. The runtime disconnected at
08:20:41; no crash was logged. This points away from the SIGUSR1-handler path
and leaves the caller of `wait_suspend()` unidentified.

is `src/wine-patches-ps4/`; the build command must pass that directory with
`--patches`. Do not use the older `prospero-win/wine/patches/` mirror, which
does not contain the startup diagnostics. The patch-series check passed.

The new trace closes the prior uncertainty about `wait_suspend()`: at the
logged checkpoints, SIGUSR1 enters ntdll's `usr1_handler`, which calls
`wait_suspend()` from its `is_inside_syscall()` branch. The sampled return
address `0x800bdad89` resolves to that handler in `ntdll.prx`. Its zero-timeout

preserves the blocked read context, not evidence that it changes the thread's
state. The klog contains the expected `select-result` and ntdll traces, but no
`wineserver sigusr1-send` records despite those strings being in the built
server PRX; the origin of the repeated SIGUSR1 delivery remains unresolved.

#### ORBW00053 sampled-thread request trace (2026-10-17)

The 0927 package reproduced the same spinning-dots stall. Its SIGUSR1 snapshot
reported TEB `7ffd8000` with `REQ_select` (`req=29`) completed (`stage=0`,
`got=64`), while the independent native PC sampler reported TEB `7ffda000` in
`read(fd=0x20, ..., 0x40)`. At the time these looked like different threads.
The 0928 run shows these are the paired 32-bit and 64-bit TEB addresses for
the same WOW64 thread (Wine tid `0x24`); the SIGUSR1 record simply captured the
thread after its select reply, while SIGPROF caught it during the next reply
read. The 0927 runtime connection ended at 09:11:28 without a crash record.

#### ORBW00053 Prospero thread-directed signal fix (2026-10-17)

The corrected 0930 package was installed. Its first launch returned to the
launcher before Wine startup; the next launch at 09:52:59 reached native WoW64
and reproduced the familiar spinning-dots hang. At 09:54:16, the sampled
thread was still blocked reading a 64-byte reply from fd 32 at native RIP
`0x8000027da`; it was cycling `REQ_select` (request 29), with no frames or
audio. This is the same stall, and the capture is sufficient; do not spend
another launch confirming it.

The trace led to a concrete portability gap in Wine's `server/ptrace.c`:
`tkill()` only uses `thr_kill2()` on FreeBSD builds. Prospero is not defined as
FreeBSD in this build, and `HAVE_THR_KILL2` is unset, so the code returns
`ENOSYS` and `send_thread_signal()` falls back to process-wide `kill(pid, sig)`.
The PS4 PRX already provides `thr_kill2()` through the registered pthread
shim. Added patch `0930-server-use-prospero-thread-directed-signal.patch` to
select that shim for Prospero. This should direct suspend SIGUSR1 to the
intended Wine thread; hardware validation is still pending.

Built Wine with patch 0930, then rebuilt the PS4 PRX, RADV, FreeType and
wineps5 chain. The build's `built=True` checks passed; its final failure report
is expected for this PS4 build (the intermediate ELF has the platform shim
symbols unresolved until the PS4 PRX link, and the optional PS5 native tool is
not installed). Built the Oblivion package only through the relocatable
wrapper. Artifact:
`pkgout/orbw00053-thread-directed-signal-reloc/IV0000-ORBW00053_00-ORBW000530000000.pkg`
(445,513,728 bytes, SHA-256
`be6948ec1b855ab8e772bbafffaba22b5d676a73076927483f3101c2a5f13eed`). The
stage contains `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx`; both relocated ELF images
verify as `DYN`; the ABI-v3 `wow64native.dll` matches its pinned hash. Uploaded
to `/data/pkg/oblivion-wait-suspend-trace.pkg` (FTP `226`). Install this update
over ORBW00053 and launch the same `oblivion-gog` native profile. Check for
`pw_wine_thread_kill enter/result` and whether the hang changes.

#### ORBW00053 PS4 signal-number ABI correction (2026-10-17)

The user launched the relocatable 0930 package and it reproduced the spinning
dots. The newest native samples stayed at RIP `0x8000027da`; `frames_put=0`,
`audio=0`, and the native counters stopped at `host_calls=1481`,
`guest_calls=1482`, `syscall_calls=1128`. The repeated server response was
`STATUS_TIMEOUT` from the empty, zero-timeout `REQ_select`, with
`suspend=0`, `proc_suspend=0`, `waiting=0`, and `context=1`.

The important detail is the raw 32-byte siginfo: signo `10`, code `3`, and
address `0x7bef14c9`, with no sender PID/UID. FreeBSD defines signal 10 as
`SIGBUS` and code 3 as `BUS_OBJERR`; the PS4 SDK's `bits/signal.h` instead
labels signal 10 `SIGUSR1` and signal 7 `SIGBUS`. The compatibility header
already translated FreeBSD `SA_*` flags and `stack_t`, but had left the Linux
signal numbers in place. This makes Wine's `SIGUSR1` handler catch what the
raw record identifies as a bus/object fault. Since that handler calls
`wait_suspend()` and the server reports no suspend, it returns to the faulting
instruction and repeats. This is the leading explanation for the dots; the
faulting address still needs a follow-up capture under the corrected mapping.

The 0930 wineserver patch was a dead end for this run: neither
`wineserver sigusr1-send` nor `pw_wine_thread_kill enter/result` appeared,
despite both markers being present in the staged PRX. The normal
`send_thread_signal()` path did not send the signal, so changing `tkill()` did
not address the repeated fault. Do not make another package around 0930.

Updated `sdk/orbis-sdk-v1/orbis-compat/include/signal.h` to expose the
FreeBSD signal numbers used by the PS4 kernel, including `SIGBUS=10` and
`SIGUSR1=30`, and removed Linux-only `SIGSTKFLT`/`SIGPWR` aliases that collide
with BSD signal numbers. A local preprocessor check confirmed the key values.
The SDK directory is gitignored, so the reproducible change is preserved in
`src/wine-platform-patches-ps4/0002-orbis-compat-use-freebsd-signal-numbers.patch`
(reverse-apply check passed); reapply it if the SDK checkout is replaced.
Rebuilt `prx-ps4.sh`, `radv-prx-ps4.sh`, `freetype-prx-ps4.sh`, and
`wineps5-prx-ps4.sh`, then packaged through `build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh`.
The package contains `relocated_wine_app.prx`; both relocated ELF images are
`DYN`; ABI-v3 `wow64native.dll` remains pinned to
`bcc51a7853d10c47a1565981889834fe19e0a715fe4ddf3e88a717a07e3cf11d`.

The new package is
`pkgout/orbw00053-signal-abi-reloc/IV0000-ORBW00053_00-ORBW000530000000.pkg`,
SHA-256 `b412b298fd252303b82f2c14127e27bdb0647eb6dfa287e8dd5af5d71f844c2a`.
It was uploaded without logging in to `/data/pkg/oblivion-signal-abi.pkg`
(FTP `226`). Install it over ORBW00053 and launch the same `oblivion-gog`
native profile. If this signal diagnosis is right, signal 10 should no longer
enter `usr1_handler`; instead Wine's corrected SIGBUS path should report or
handle the original object fault. Keep the existing listeners running.

#### ORBW00053 corrected signal ABI: native fault and compat regression (2026-10-17)

The corrected package `oblivion-signal-abi-fixed.pkg` was installed and launched
with the `oblivion-gog` native profile. Unlike prior runs, the dots no longer
spin indefinitely: Wine received the signal-10 fault and converted it to a
Windows access violation. The first exception is at `0x7bef14c9`, with
`RSP=0x100021f2d0`, `CS=0x0043`, `SS=0x003b`, and error address
`0xffffffffffffffff`. `wow64native.dll` disassembly maps RVA `0x14c9` to the
`iretq` in `syscall_32to64_return`, the reset-state return path. Native
initialization succeeded (`cs32=0033 ss32=003b cs64=0043 avx=1`) and execution
reached `BTCpuSimulate`; the next unknown is the five-word IRET frame and its
selectors/flags. Do not repeat an uninstrumented Oblivion launch.

A subsequent accidental selection of `oblivion-gog` was explicitly excluded

#### ORBW00053 raw native IRET frame and selector fault (2026-10-17)

The user installed the 0932 package and launched `oblivion-gog` with native
CPU. It crashed at 11:12:00. The 0932 handler finally captured the original
fault:

```
signal=10 code=3 addr=7bef14c9 rip=7bef14c9 rsp=100021f2d0
cs=43 ss=3b flags=10203 trap=9 err=20
frame: rip=7bf2e450 cs=23 rflags=202 rsp=21fff0 ss=3b
```

`trap=9` is #GP and error `0x20` identifies selector index 4, matching the
saved return CS `0x23`. Native initialization reports PS4 compatibility
selectors `CS=0x33`, `SS=0x3b`. The return frame therefore contains a different
CS than the PS4 selector reported at initialization. This is strong evidence
that the `iretq` failure is in the WOW64 return frame/selector path, before
Oblivion can render; it does not implicate the game or Mesa. The crash then
produced secondary SIGSEGV/SIGILL faults during process teardown; those are
downstream noise.

#### ORBW00053 fault-page query result (2026-10-17)

The 0933 package reproduced the same AV at 11:42:06. The new Wine-side query
reported:

```
addr=004a8330 eip=004a8330 query=00000000 base=004A8000 alloc=00400000
size=580000 state=1000 protect=20 alloc_protect=80 type=1000000
```

This is a successful query of committed (`MEM_COMMIT`) image memory
(`MEM_IMAGE`) with `PAGE_EXECUTE_READ` protection. The local Oblivion image
disassembles `0x004a8330` as `push 0xffffffff`, a valid function prologue; that
instruction writes to the stack, while Wine reports a read AV whose bad address
is the same as EIP. Thus the query rules out an absent/uncommitted image page
and ordinary no-read protection, but the exception address/access type and
instruction semantics remain inconsistent. Do not infer a game bug or alter
the page protection from this evidence alone.

ps5log listeners were left running. Next, reproduce once and inspect the
captured dump's native fault context, guest context, and mapping around
`0x004a8330` to resolve the read/EIP mismatch.

#### ORBW00053 executable-page protection test (2026-10-17)

The captured raw x86 page-fault error at the repeated fault is `err=0x15`:
present page, user-mode access, instruction fetch. That matches the hardware
attempting to fetch code at `0x004a8330`, while the 0933 Wine query reports the
same committed image page as `PAGE_EXECUTE_READ`. The fault is therefore a
host page-table execution-permission mismatch, rather than a missing PE page
or flexible-memory exhaustion.

### Native WoW64 SIGSYS dispatch fix (2026-10-17)

The latest crash was no longer the reset-state `iretq` or executable-page
protection. Wine logged `syscall fault in syscall 0x2c (ntdll #44)` at
`NtQueryDirectoryFile`, followed by the raw PS4 `SIGSYS`. Inspection of the
PS4 Wine source showed that the native syscall-trap handler (`sigsys_handler`)
was compiled and installed only under `__APPLE__` or `__linux__`. The PS4 SDK
defines `__PROSPERO__`, not either of those macros, so native WoW64 syscall
traps bypassed Wine's dispatcher and reached the early fatal handler.

Added `src/wine-patches-ps4/0934-ntdll-route-prospero-sigsys-to-wine.patch` to
include `__PROSPERO__` in both guards, and
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0042-build-wine-ps5-extend-patch-series-0934.patch`
to extend the build patch-series ceiling. `--check-patches` passed through
0934. The Wine build wrapper returned 1 because this checkout lacks the
optional `ps5-native-tool`, but its required outputs reported `built=True`,
including `dlls/ntdll/ntdll.so`; then the required PS4 PRX chain completed in
order: `prx-ps4.sh`, RADV, FreeType, and wineps5.

Built the Oblivion package through the required relocatable path with ABI-v3
`wow64native.dll` SHA-256
`d7029105cae5d9ba7eecb16b33b02fff8d4c0d7dba7801ab4cd817053a88ce36`. Both
stub and relocated Wine image verify as ELF `DYN`. Package:
`pkgout/oblivion-prospero-sigsys-reloc/IV0000-GLDS10056_00-GLDS100560000000.pkg`,
SHA-256
`7413360533b4ac984219e6f2442325b1fca6530ead804b53499113173cbcad7d`.
Uploaded successfully (FTP `226`) as
`/data/pkg/oblivion-prospero-sigsys.pkg`. Install/update GLDS10056 and launch
the same `oblivion-gog` profile. This is a functional dispatcher fix; the next
run will show whether Oblivion advances or exposes another unsupported native
WoW64 path. Existing log listeners should remain running.

#### SIGSYS dispatch retest and executable mmap correction (2026-10-17)

The user installed and launched GLDS10056, saw spinning dots, and then closed
the app cleanly from the PS4 menu. GoldHEN did not report `notifyAppCrash`, so
there is no coredump; the capture watcher remains armed for `ORBW00053`.

The new klog/ps5log show the original `NtQueryDirectoryFile` path now succeeds
(`NtQueryDirectoryFile => 0`), confirming the 0934 handler routes at least that
native WoW64 syscall into Wine. Oblivion then reaches its known guest fetch
fault at `EIP=0x004a8330` (`SIGSEGV`, `err=0x15`). Wine attempts to start
`winedbg`, but `NtCreateUserProcess` is not supported on this runtime; the
process stays in spinning-dot state until the user closes the title. The
subsequent `SIGSYS` report is a different `win32u #3770` call, followed by
repeated `STATUS_ILLEGAL_INSTRUCTION` at
`libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0xb17d9`. Do not call this a shell crash or claim a
dump was captured.

The missing executable-protection call is explained by Wine's protection
cache: `counted_mmap()` marked the requested `PROT_EXEC` as known immediately
after a successful mmap, so `set_vprot()` considered it unchanged and skipped
`mprotect`. This is unsafe on PS4, where the requested mmap protection did not
produce a working instruction-fetch mapping. Patch
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0935-ntdll-force-prospero-exec-protection-after-mmap.patch`
now calls the existing PS4 `__wine_ps5_mprotect` path on executable mmaps
before caching the protection. The full ordered patch check passes through
0935; the Wine wrapper still exits 1 only because optional
`ps5-native-tool` is absent, while `ntdll.so` and the other required targets
report `built=True`. The PS4 PRX chain completed afterward.

New relocatable package, retaining ABI-v3 DLL SHA-256
`d7029105cae5d9ba7eecb16b33b02fff8d4c0d7dba7801ab4cd817053a88ce36`:
`pkgout/oblivion-exec-mmap-protection-reloc/IV0000-GLDS10056_00-GLDS100560000000.pkg`,
SHA-256 `2ff68be83def846df36051932119d8f2fd740d559fd66fc446cceaaf72cd97b0`.
Both generated images verify as ELF `DYN`, and the staged `ntdll.prx`
contains the executable-mprotect trace. Uploaded successfully (FTP `226`) to
`/data/pkg/oblivion-exec-mmap-protection.pkg`. Install/update GLDS10056 and
launch the same `oblivion-gog` profile. Check for `wine-ps4: exec mprotect`

protection. Leave the active log and crash watchers running.

#### PS4 native single-step fault identified and handled (2026-10-17)

The user installed the 0938 SIGILL diagnostic package and launched
`oblivion-gog`. The first records identify why the same fault loop persists:

- Native `SIGSYS` at `RIP=0x8000028bc` has `RAX=0x2cafdec2`; 0937 passes it
  through.
- The immediate follow-on signal reports `trap=1` and `RFLAGS=0x10202` (TF
  set), with `inside_syscall=1`. On x86, trap vector 1 is `#DB` single-step,
  not invalid opcode. It arrives at Wine's `segv_handler`; that handler does
  not handle `TRAP_x86_TRCTRAP`, so its default case mislabeled this as
  `STATUS_ILLEGAL_INSTRUCTION` and `handle_syscall_fault()` replayed a stale
  frame. The first such stop is in `libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0xb17d9`; the next
  repeats in `ntdll.prx+0x9a9c9`.
- This is why the 0938 trace matters: it confirms both TF and
  `inside_syscall=1`, rather than merely an unusual opcode or bad address.

Added patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0939-ntdll-consume-prospero-syscall-single-step.patch`.
For a `TRAP_x86_TRCTRAP` with TF set while RSP is inside the Wine syscall stack,
it clears TF, runs `leave_handler()`, and resumes at the next host instruction.
Other single-step cases retain Wine's existing behavior. Patch-series extension
0047 accepts 0939. The full Wine build reports all required targets
`built=True` (only the known optional PS5-native-tool link is unavailable), and
the PS4 PRX/RADV/FreeType/wineps5 chain completed.

#### Orbis raw #DB vector correction (2026-10-17)

The 14:34 launch was captured by the existing listeners and crashed at the
same fault. Its first SIGILL records again showed `trap=1`, TF set in
`flags=0x10202`, a non-null TEB, and `inside_syscall=1`; the 0939
`Consuming Prospero single-step` line did not appear. The cause is now
identified in the comparison itself: Orbis puts the raw x86 exception vector
`1` in its signal context, while Wine's FreeBSD compatibility header defines
`T_TRCTRAP=10`. Patch 0939 compared against Wine's mapped value, so it could
never match the PS4 event.

advances the local ceiling. The ordered patch check passes through 0940. Wine's

#### 0942 crash location and 0943 raw-trap fix (2026-10-17)

The 0942 core was captured at
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10056_1792276109.sorbisdmp` (187,391 bytes; SHA-256
`88d024761e7633914c11a29c1e1ad3b3c1c3e3b3486a44de57ca604230cf4cee`). Its
runtime log shows `trap=1`, `flags=0x10202`, and `inside=1`, but `is_step=0`.
Correction: `0x10202` does **not** contain the x86 TF bit `0x100`; TF is already
clear when this PS4 event reaches Wine. Wine's mapped trap value is 10, so the
0942 guard rejected the event on both the trap comparison and TF check. This is
still the native WoW64 signal/syscall-return path. The handler replays the
syscall frame, and a later Wine thread faults in `ntdll.prx` on address `0x70`.
The longer time to failure is consistent with the repeated fault/recovery loop
and slow title startup; it does not establish that execution advanced further
into Oblivion.

Patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0943-ntdll-accept-raw-orbis-single-step-trap.patch`
accepts either raw vector 1 or Wine's mapped trap constant, but still required
TF for both. The 0943 runtime marker confirmed `trap=1`, `flags=0x10202`,
`inside=1`, and `is_step=0`; it therefore reproduced the loop. Its final records
show repeated illegal-instruction faults at `ntdll.prx+0x9adb9`, followed by a
system-thread access violation at `ntdll.prx+0x84ab3` reading `0x70`. The core
was not recoverable: the old klog TCP connection had been stale since the
console's shutdown, and GoldHEN had already removed the dump before the
connection was refreshed. The listener has since been reconnected.

Patch `src/wine-patches-ps4/0944-ntdll-accept-orbis-single-step-without-tf.patch`
was built from an incorrect interpretation of `trap=1` as the raw x86 #DB
vector. The PS4's `mc_trapno` follows the FreeBSD trap numbering in
`src/freebsd-compat/machine/trap.h`: `T_PRIVINFLT=1` and `T_TRCTRAP=10`.
With `flags=0x10202` (TF clear), trap 1 is a privileged-instruction fault, not
a single-step. 0944's broader predicate is therefore wrong and must not be
retained as a fix. The ordered patch check passed and the PS4 PRX chain built,
but the hardware test below disproves the behavior.

#### 0944 test result (2026-10-18)

0944 was installed/launched. Its runtime logs record `trap=1`, `flags=0x10202`,
`is_step=1`, `inside=1`; because trap 1 is `T_PRIVINFLT`, that predicate
misclassified the privileged-instruction fault and `segv_handler` swallowed it.
The same fault recurred millions of times at RIP `0x8001417d9`, with syscall
frame `0x2c` and frame RIP `0x6ffffff3f1d4`. The app remained alive for over
three minutes, but
`PW_WINE64` reported `frames_put=0`, while `PW_NATIVE_PROFILE` counters stayed
fixed at `host_calls=1613`, `guest_calls=1614`, `syscall_calls=1200`. No new
`handle_syscall_fault` loop or app crash appeared during the captured interval.
Conclusion: 0944 masks a recurring privileged-instruction fault without
advancing execution; the earlier immediate crash became an infinite fault
loop. The next patch should identify the instruction bytes and loaded image at
RIP `0x8001417d9`, then correctly dispatch or fix that instruction fault. Logs are in
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261017-081553.log` and
`build-ps4-logs/ps4-klog-20261017-resumed.log`.

#### 0945 PE-refresh hardware result (2026-10-18)

The PE-refresh package launched Oblivion, which reached the same guest fault
about 20 seconds after startup. At 09:53:19, Wine dispatched
`EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION`, read access (`info[0]=0`) to `0x004a8330`, with
guest `EIP=0x004a8330`. The patched i386 ntdll reported a successful virtual
memory query: page base `0x004a8000`, allocation base `0x00400000`, region size
`0x580000`, `MEM_COMMIT`, `PAGE_EXECUTE_READ`, allocation protection
`PAGE_EXECUTE_WRITECOPY`, and `MEM_IMAGE`. The saved console `Oblivion.exe`
(SHA-256 `035dba38fddd325b3ca18afdaacd086eecf7a8a33fce1abdb057e5cd831e1ee4`)
disassembles at this address as RVA `0xA8330`, inside `.text`, with bytes
`6a ff` (`push -1`) at a normal function prologue. The page is present and
readable in Wine's map; this does not establish the actual hardware page-table
permission, and the read-access exception still disagrees with the instruction
and queried protection.

Wine then failed to start `winedbg`. The log entered repeated SIGILL/UD2 faults
at `libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0xb17d9`, followed by a system-thread AV in
`ntdll.prx+0x84e33` reading `0x70`; `frames_put` remained zero. GoldHEN reported
crash dump `GLDS10056_1792342505.sorbisdmp`, but the existing klog connection
had not appended since 09:37. A second klog connection recovered the notice
after GoldHEN's crash reporter had deleted the dump; one direct RETR of its
exact path returned FTP 550. Runtime trace is in
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261017-081553.log`; the late system-log capture is
`build-ps4-logs/ps4-klog-20261018-fresh.log`.

`wine-ps4: exec mprotect` result before the failure. If both libkernel and libc
protection calls succeed, the next investigation should focus on the raw
native fault context/SEH transition, not repeat the image-map query or change
the execute-permission policy.

#### 0945 repeat launch and captured crash (2026-10-18)

The user relaunched the installed package at 10:46:39. About 20 seconds later,
at 10:46:59.981, Wine again dispatched a read `EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION`
(`info[0]=0`) at `0x004a8330`, with guest EIP `0x004a8330`. The refreshed
i386 ntdll emitted the page-map query this time: query succeeded, page base
`0x004a8000`, allocation base `0x00400000`, size `0x580000`, state
`MEM_COMMIT`, protection `PAGE_EXECUTE_READ`, allocation protection
`PAGE_EXECUTE_WRITECOPY`, type `MEM_IMAGE`. This reproduces the same
read-vs-instruction-fetch/protection contradiction; it is not a new fault
location.

Wine again attempted to start `winedbg`, which is absent from the prefix. After
that, the runtime repeatedly faulted on UD2 at
`libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0xb17d9`, then at `ntdll.prx+0x9b139` while handling
native syscall `0x2c` (ntdll syscall 44). The process made no frames
(`frames_put=0`) while the diagnostic sink count climbed rapidly. At
10:48:00.845 GoldHEN reported an application crash and started a coredump; the
fresh watcher retrieved it before cleanup:
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10056_1792345680.sorbisdmp` (191,283 bytes, SHA-256
`5097cf9333c48f73928fe5008064c4b57c223d249b3e8d0c0eb801b3a40ed6b1`). The
runtime trace is in `build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261017-081553.log`, and the fresh
klog is in `build-ps4-logs/ps4-klog-20261018-fresh.log`.

The periodic runtime counters show `mprotect=875, skipped=749, faults=0`
immediately before the guest AV, then `mprotect=880, skipped=750, faults=1`
after it. The expected per-call `wine-ps4: exec mprotect` lines were not found
in either captured stream, so these counters alone do not identify which
protection call failed or whether the final attempted page protection took
effect. Do not claim that executable-page permission is proven fixed. The next
useful step is to inspect the captured dump with a compatible Sorbis dump
reader if one is available, and otherwise add a narrow trace at the point the
guest AV is converted to `Unhandled page fault` so the original exception
context and the failure in `winedbg` startup can be distinguished. Do not spend
another launch cycle on this same package without a diagnostic change.

#### 0948 hardware result and 0949 effective-page query (2026-10-18)

0948 reached the same image fault. Its raw pre-hook klog frame is:

```text
sig=11 code=2 addr=4a8330 rip=4a8330 rsp=21fea4 cs=33 ss=3b flags=10202 trap=12 err=15
```

The page-fault error code `0x15` has present, user, and instruction-fetch bits
set, with the write bit clear. This is a CPU instruction-fetch protection
fault on a present user page; Wine's earlier “read AV” classification was not
the hardware access type. The corresponding GoldHEN dump was captured as
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10056_1792349890.sorbisdmp` (184,599 bytes, SHA-256
`9a75fa41405283052357618785d8b728f2ed441fbbb77914b824776355b1d6eb`).

Patch 0947 already established that `sceKernelMprotect` returns success for
the image range covering `0x004a8330`, but that return alone does not establish
the page table's effective permissions. The new PS4 native diagnostic in
`src/native/pw_wine_dmem_glue_ps4.c` now calls `sceKernelVirtualQuery` on that
exact address after the covering executable-protection call and logs the
reported range, CPU protection bits, memory type, flags, and region name.
Interpret the next result as follows: query failure means the kernel cannot
describe the fault address; a successful result without CPU execute permission
means the mapping did not become executable; a successful result with CPU
execute permission alongside the same hardware instruction-fetch fault points
to a discrepancy below this API-level mapping report.

be loaded through an executable alias.”

#### 0954 result and 0955 anonymous execute-permission probe (2026-10-18)

The 0954 run reached the original failure again: a present-page instruction
fetch fault at `0x004a8330` (`sig=11`, page-fault error `0x15`). The dump was
automatically retrieved as
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10056_1792359737.sorbisdmp` (187,484 bytes, SHA-256
`eb18794c259f3cc0f69517816041623ec6e2ff7104d1714dad95c687736ddf72`).
The relocated eboot called both JIT functions successfully through `Dlsym`,
but `sceKernelJitCreateSharedMemory(0, 0x4000, 7, &fd)` returned
`-2147352575` (`0x80020001`, `EPERM`), so no JIT object was created and Wine
continued normally. This matches published PS4 kernel research: the JIT shared
memory syscall is restricted to JIT compiler processes, and ordinary apps get
EPERM ([syscall reference](https://www.psdevwiki.com/ps4/Syscalls),
[CTurt's explanation](https://cturt.github.io/ps4-3.html)). The standard app
cannot use that path without changing the process privilege policy.

0955 removes the JIT attempt and replaces it with a non-executing permission
probe on two disposable 16-KiB anonymous mappings in eboot startup. It records
the mapping state, requests RWX with both `sceKernelMprotect` and libc
`mprotect`, queries effective protections after each, then restores RW and
unmaps. A second anonymous mapping requests RWX directly and is queried but
never executed. This isolates process-level executable-memory policy from the
PE image mapping while avoiding another diagnostic-triggered crash.

0960 tests the now-supported alternative: preserve the contents of only the
faulting 16-KiB page, release its direct-memory backing, map that page as
ordinary anonymous flexible memory at the same address, register it as a
caller-owned page in the direct-memory tracker, and let the existing RX request
apply. The startup RWX probe already showed flexible anonymous memory can be
executable. This is a bounded migration experiment, not another protection-bit
variant. The targeted klog record reports migration success or the failing
stage; the existing post-mprotect `sceKernelVirtualQuery` record must then show
execute permission before we treat the page as fixed. The original contents
are copied back if remapping or tracker adoption fails.

repackaged through `build-relocatable-wine-ps4.sh`. The packaged
`sce_module/ntdll.prx` now contains the `exec-page flex-migrate` markers; both
app ELFs verify as `DYN`. The final artifact is

handler only when RX is confirmed. Signal return should retry the interrupted
instruction. The experiment is limited to eight attempts; failed verification
falls through to normal Wine fault handling. Patch:
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0954-ntdll-repair-oblivion-rx-in-signal-handler.patch`.

#### 0971 result and 0972 effective-execute fallback (2026-10-18)

The 0971 run reached the same fault and was captured as

important comparison is now measured: the faulting mapping
`0x004a4000-0x004a8000` is direct-memory-backed and remains read-only after an
RX protection call returns zero. The adjacent `0x004a8000-0x004ac000` mapping
is flexible memory; it was observed both read-only and RX, and our existing
heartbeat's RX repair changed it back to RX. This points to a backing-memory
class difference for the persistent fault, rather than a need to keep adding
addresses to the retry list. The reason that page's earlier protection state
changes is still unknown; our heartbeat and signal retry are known writers on
`0x004a8000`.

0972 replaces the one-address migration experiment with an effective-permission
fallback in `__wine_ps5_mprotect`: executable requests are applied per PS4
16-KiB granule and checked with `sceKernelVirtualQuery`. If a fully direct-
memory-backed granule still lacks execute permission, the code preserves its
contents, moves just that granule to flexible memory, retries RX, and verifies
the result. It does not migrate pages whose query already reports RX or whose
backing is not fully direct memory. A bounded `exec-protect-verify build=0972`
line is emitted for failed checks and migrations.

#### 0973 result and 0974 bounded executable-range fallback (2026-10-18)

0973 did not crash, but Oblivion never produced a frame or audio. It remained
alive for about seven minutes while Wine continued working through a single
executable-protection request (`call=1194`). The direct-memory fallback
migrated thousands of 16-KiB pages, progressing from `0x019d0000` through at
least `0x119cc000`; after roughly 3,451 per-page checks, migration began
failing at `0x118f0000` with `mmap` returning `EINVAL` (not an allocation
failure). Flexible memory free fell from about 228 MiB to 63 MiB during the
stall. No coredump was produced. The user closed the run from the PS4 menu;
klog records the wineserver process/thread teardown at 19:39:22.

The native PC sampler repeatedly caught the active thread at
`0x800001ddc` (libkernel wrapper for syscall `0x259`), with no change in the
Wine transition counters. The ntdll sample at 19:34:05 recorded
`REQ_select` (`req=29`) complete, but that single sample does not establish
that select caused the stall. The decisive 0973 result is that fallback
migration ran on an oversized executable range and made the launch stall
worse; do not retest 0973.

0974 caps page migration to executable-protection requests no larger than
16 MiB. Larger requests retain the previous direct-memory protection path and
emit a bounded `exec-range build=0974` record with the request address, size,
protection, caller, and whether fallback was skipped. This keeps the measured
Oblivion image request (about 6.2 MiB) eligible while preventing a broad
request from walking and migrating hundreds of MiB. It is a containment and
measurement change; it does not yet explain why Wine issued call 1194 or
prove the title will launch.

#### 0974 hardware result and 0975 SIGPROF gate (2026-10-18)

The 0974 run launched at 19:47 and exited about 45 seconds later, before any
frame or audio. Wine had loaded through `user32`, `win32u`, `cfgmgr32`, and
`hid` while reporting `frames_put=0`; flexible memory remained about 334 MiB
and `dmem_failures=0`. The oversized executable-range path was not reached,
so 0974 does not validate the 16 MiB cap.

The first fatal event is a repeat of the already-known native signal/TLS
collision, now with its trigger identified. At 19:47:45.466 the process took
SIGBUS (10), a general-protection fault at `libkernel.sprx+0xc742`. The
matching saved libkernel disassembly shows that instruction is `movl (%rax),
%eax` in the signal-delivery path. The delivery helper had been called with
`rdi=27` (SIGPROF); its result was the noncanonical pointer
`0x002436087fff1f88`. The preceding helper reads `FS:0x10` and returns that
value plus `0x188`, so the pointer contains the guest TEB's adjacent 32-bit
fields (`0x7fff1e00` and `0x00243608`) interpreted together as an Orbis TCB
pointer. The native PC sampler itself sends `pthread_kill(..., SIGPROF)` every
two seconds; its last successful sample was at 19:47:43, immediately before
this fault. The SIGBUS is therefore caused by our diagnostic signal, not by
the executable-range cap or an observed memory shortage. Dump:
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10056_1792378065.sorbisdmp` (133,244 bytes; SHA-256
`33df103d233f9e51cbefa4c49596e00866ed1f33145c749406bd2b10f12adefc`).

Updated the native PC sampler in
`src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0038-wow64native-sample-native-pc.patch` and the
applied source `prospero-win/wine/wow64native/unix.c`: synthetic SIGPROF is
now disabled unless `PW_NATIVE_PC=1` is explicitly present. The app launcher
does not set that variable. Native CPU execution and the signal-free
CPU-time/transition counters remain enabled. This avoids the profiler's
known signal path while keeping the actual native-title test intact; it does
not fix signal/TLS handling generally, so a signal raised naturally by the
game may still expose the same underlying limitation.

samples are not trustworthy for identifying hot threads: many distinct TEBs,
including ones whose host/guest transition counters barely changed, report
nearly identical ~2-second deltas per ~2-second interval. Do not infer a

#### 2026-10-19: gameplay performance sample and native-profile watcher crash

The next 0977 run was left open long enough to reach Oblivion gameplay. The
user observed about 3 FPS with the GPU estimate around 0–5%. This makes GPU
saturation an unlikely explanation for the low frame rate. It does not by
itself distinguish CPU-bound game work from waits elsewhere. During the earlier
loading screen, the DXVK HUD showed 0.3 FPS and an estimated 31% GPU load; that
was a different phase and should not be substituted for the gameplay reading.
Runtime `cpu_ms` continued increasing by about 1.5 CPU-seconds per wall-second
near the end of the run, but the per-thread CPU samples from the diagnostic
watcher are not reliable enough to identify a hot thread.

The run then crashed at 08:02:47 PDT. The coredump was captured locally as
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10056_1792422168.sorbisdmp` (244,853 bytes); the capture
attempts and final success are recorded in
`build-ps4-logs/glds10056-coredump-capture-20261019.log`. Runtime fault:

```text
0xc0000005 at /app0/win/wine/lib/wine/x86_64-unix/wow64native.so+0x1a4d8
fault address 0x9d6; RIP 0x8010e24d8
```

The address resolves against the packaged `wow64native.prx` companion's
matching `wow64native.shared.elf` to `profile_read` in `wine/wow64native/unix.c`.
The faulting instruction dereferences the profile pointer loaded from
`pw_native_thread_state`. The native profiling watcher retains pointers to
thread-local state while it samples threads; concurrent thread teardown can
leave one stale. This is therefore a crash in our profiling instrumentation,
not evidence that the game or native CPU execution itself caused the crash.
The next build should fix the watcher lifetime race or disable that watcher;
do not repeat the same build and diagnostic configuration. Keep the coredump
and logs as evidence.

Current logs are `build-ps4-logs/ps4-klog-20261019-062855.log`,
`build-ps4-logs/ps4-udp-20261019-062855.log`, and
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261019-062855.log`. Listeners were left running.

whole startup delay and is not an interrupted wait.

0978: 07:35:27.988 -> 07:45:32.009 = 10m04s

I checked the package and live prefix to identify those failures. The PS4 Wine
build is configured with `--without-gstreamer`, and 0981 contains neither
`winegstreamer.dll` nor `winegstreamer.so`; its Quartz PE DLL is present. In
this Wine source, `dlls/quartz/parser.c` implements the AVI splitter, MPEG-1
splitter, and Wave Parser by calling `CoCreateInstance()` on the three
`winegstreamer` CLSIDs. Quartz still registers those wrapper filters in
`DllRegisterServer()`. The live prefix's `system.reg` contains the normal
Quartz filter registrations but no registrations for those WineGStreamer
CLSID values. Thus the logged `0x80040154` errors are explained by the build
omitting the classes that Quartz's wrappers try to instantiate; they are not
evidence of a bad game-data path. The failure/retry pattern may be contributing
to the slow media/startup path, but a file-level `RenderFile` trace is still
needed to identify the caller and asset before changing filter behavior. The
current screen telemetry remains at four captured GDI frames and zero Vulkan
frames shown, so the apparent launch has not yet been verified as gameplay.

#### 2026-10-19: loaded-game FPS rise and hard render stall

The next run reused the installed 0981 package and the same native

menu it reached about 60 presents/s. After the save loaded, the present trace
briefly reached 56–58/s, matching the user's on-screen 50+ FPS observation.
It did not sustain that rate: later 120-present intervals varied from about
35 to 51/s, with intermittent slower intervals.
The PS4 crash-report UI still labels the app `Oblivion Vulkan Present Trace
0977`, but the runtime heartbeat includes the `minflt`/`majflt`/`inblock`
counters introduced in 0981; the test package was 0981.

The game stopped presenting after Vulkan present `#6600` at 12:22:40.371.
Initially this looked like a game-thread stall, but the klog shows the inner
Wine/game process actually faulted at 12:23:00.474. It received SIGSEGV
(`code=1`, read fault at address `0x4`) at `RIP=0x802635e23`; wineserver then
violently terminated the game's threads. The outer launcher/runtime remained
alive and kept emitting stale heartbeats until the user killed it from the PS4
menu around 12:30:55. Thus the apparent long stall after the last present was
the wrapper left running after the inner process had faulted.

The module map places RIP in `/app0/sce_module/libvulkan.prx`, at offset
`0xffde23`. That package's PRX SHA-256 matched the PRX built from
`prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/prx/libvulkan.shared.elf`; `addr2line` resolves the
offset to Mesa's `linear_alloc_child`, and disassembly identifies the faulting
instruction as `movl 0x4(%rdi), %ecx` at function offset `+0x13`. Given the
reported fault address `0x4`, `rdi` was null: the allocator received a null
`linear_ctx` and faulted reading `ctx->offset`. This is a confirmed Mesa-side
null-context call, but its caller and why the context was null remain unknown.
The `PW_WINE64` counters had been flat for several minutes (`majflt=2375`,
`minflt=105516`, `inblock` about 6035, audio unchanged), so this fault is not
evidence of a storage stall or swapping.

The input `refused` counter rose from 105 to 371 during the stall, while the
separate pad mapper continued to report mapped button events just before exit.
That correlation is worth preserving, but it does not establish that rejected
keyboard/mouse injection caused the render stall.

The watcher captured
`build-ps4-logs/GLDS10056_1792422168.sorbisdmp` (244,853 bytes; SHA-256
`b74abc04f9af906f1e51a7ab0ddd575288d7f71b0feb114265904d53fd125810`). The
matching klog says `IsProcessKill=True` and records the user's menu kill; this
is a dump generated during process termination, not evidence that the game
raised an exception on its own. Do not label this as a spontaneous game crash.
The `.sorbisdmp` is retained, but no local decoder for its `FACECS` format has
been found yet. Capture and logs are in
`build-ps4-logs/glds10056-coredump-capture-20261019.log`,
`build-ps4-logs/ps4-klog-20261019-062855.log`, and
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261019-062855.log`.

The termination dump is from the later outer-app menu kill and does not contain
the inner SIGSEGV's useful fault frame. To identify the null allocator caller,

The performance evidence remains separate: the loaded save briefly reached

#### Startup latency takes priority over the Mesa crash (2026-10-19)

The user has explicitly prioritized Oblivion's startup/load latency over the
later Mesa crash: each full test can take roughly 30 minutes, which makes
repeated crash-only runs too expensive. Keep the Mesa null-context fix in the
current package, but do not spend the next iteration on reproducing that fault
unless startup work has produced a shorter, useful test window.

What is established about latency:

- From `Oblivion.exe` start to its first full-size Vulkan swapchain, the recent
  runs take about 12 minutes. The interval from DXVK's “Using 6 compiler
  threads” marker to the swapchain is about 10 minutes, but this is correlation,
  not proof that shader compilation causes it.
- The `GPL=True` and `GPL=False` A/B runs had effectively the same startup
  duration. Do not repeat that experiment or assume GPL will solve this.
- Measured synchronous `NtReadFile` work was only about 0.4–0.63 seconds total
  across roughly 15 MiB; the longest read was under 50 ms. This makes ordinary
  synchronous reads an unlikely explanation, but does not measure mapped-file
  page-in or prove that storage is irrelevant.
- Process CPU time accumulated at about two CPU-seconds per wall second during
  the long phase. Major faults stopped increasing at 2,375, available memory
  remained healthy, and there was no evidence of swap pressure. The process is
  doing substantial CPU work; the existing counters do not identify its code.
- The DirectShow `0x80040154` filter retries were recorded around 10:21 in one
  run, after first full-size output at 10:11. They may affect a later media
  transition, but they do not explain the preceding ten-minute startup delay.
- The old per-thread profiler patch `0037` is not a viable next step: its
  reported `cpu_delta_ns` advanced by roughly two seconds for many distinct
  threads, including threads whose transition counters barely moved. Treat
  those samples as invalid; do not rebuild or enable that watcher again.

For the next session, optimize the test around the actual startup milestone:
use the first full-size game output (about 12 minutes after process start) as
the observation cutoff, and do not wait through save loading or gameplay when
the experiment only concerns startup. Avoid another generic “collect more
logs” package. First inspect the existing 0980 wait-loop evidence and the
current native startup path for a specific, testable cause; make one targeted
change whose outcome can be judged at that first-output milestone. Preserve
the current package and logs so the Mesa crash can be diagnosed later without
making it the gate for startup work.

#### R2-correlated termination after gameplay (2026-10-19)

The user closed the app from the PS4 menu after the game stopped presenting.
This was a manual close, not a confirmed spontaneous console crash. The core
watcher did not report a new dump; its capture log still refers to an older
dump. Do not claim a core was captured for this run.

The last input immediately before Wine's termination path is identifiable:
at 13:44:49.798, controller mask `0x200` was pressed and translated to keyboard
`C` (VK `0x43`); it was released at 13:44:50.052. The input table in
`prospero-win/src/pw_game_profile.c` identifies mask `0x200` as R2, and
`src/profiles/oblivion-gog.profile` maps `r2 = c`. At 13:44:50.448, Wine thread
`0064` logged `seh:raise (22)`. At 13:44:50.701 it faulted on the intentional
`ud2` at `libSceLibcInternal.sprx+0xb17d9`; the recorded Wine syscall frame
was `NtTerminateProcess` (`ntdll #44`, syscall `0x2c`), though patch 0876 warns
that this frame can be stale and it does not prove the fault occurred inside
that syscall. The repeated
fault then reached `ntdll.prx+0x9c369`, the intentional `ud2` after
`abort_process()` calls `_exit()`, and produced a rapidly repeating SIGSYS /
SIGILL log loop until the user closed the app.

Interpretation: R2 immediately preceded the game/Wine exception and is a
plausible trigger, but the log proves only correlation. The `ud2` sites are
non-returning sentinels in the termination path, not evidence of a bad GPU
instruction or that R2 itself executes invalid code. The original exception
and why the PS4 `_exit`/termination path returns into those sentinels remain
unresolved. The PS5 log grew to 443 MiB because the same SIGSYS/SIGILL path was
logged repeatedly; preserve it, but bound repeated fault diagnostics in any
future build so one termination cannot flood the receiver. Current capture:
`build-ps4-logs/ps5log-20261019-062855.log` (443 MiB),
`build-ps4-logs/ps4-klog-20261019-062855.log` (48 MiB), and
`build-ps4-logs/glds10056-coredump-capture-20261019.log` (no new dump).
Listeners remain running.

For a controlled input check, a temporary profile-only A/B is now staged on the
console at `/data/prospero-win/profiles/oblivion-gog.profile`: it is identical
to `src/profiles/oblivion-gog.profile` except `r2 = none`. GoldHEN FTP returned
`226 File action successful` for the direct STOR. The matching local copy is
`codex_scratch/oblivion-r2-ab/oblivion-gog.profile`. No package rebuild or
reinstall is needed; start the same GLDS10056 app from the launcher so it
reloads the profile. At the comparable gameplay point, press R2 once. If the
exception/termination loop is absent, that supports the R2-to-C input path as
the trigger; if it remains, R2 is not sufficient. Restore the original profile

#### Native slow-load call-ID diagnostic package 0982 (2026-10-19)

The user closed the prior run after the slow gameplay phase; this next test is
for startup latency only. The evidence still says the roughly 11–12 minute
pre-display phase is CPU-active: around 30,000 native guest-to-host transitions
per second, about two CPU-seconds per wall second, while major faults plateau
and block-input counts remain comparatively low. Ordinary synchronous
`NtReadFile` time measured earlier was under one second total. Those counters do
not identify which Wine work is hot, so package 0982 adds a bounded sample of
dispatched call IDs instead of another broad log trace.

Patch `src/prospero-win-patches-ps4/0067-wow64native-sample-dispatched-call-ids.patch`
updates the native WoW64 profile shared block and samples one Windows syscall
ID and one Wine Unix-call ID every 1024 dispatches. The existing profiler
reports the newest 16 IDs per category every two seconds in
`PW_NATIVE_CALL_IDS`. These are rolling samples, not full counts: repetition
among the recent IDs identifies a likely hot dispatch family. Syscall values
come from the guest syscall dispatch; Unix IDs pack the module handle in the
high 16 bits and call ordinal in the low 16 bits. Resolve Unix ordinals through
`__wine_unix_call_funcs` in the module named by the handle. This sampling does
not enable the unsafe asynchronous PC/signal sampler.

Package `pkgout/oblivion-callids-0982-reloc/IV0000-GLDS10056_00-GLDS100560000000.pkg`
is a relocatable build using the same GLDS10056 title ID and Oblivion profile.
It includes `sce_module/relocated_wine_app.prx`; both app ELF images were
verified as `DYN`. During the staging audit, 0982 initially picked up a newer
Mesa `libvulkan.prx`. That unrelated change was removed: both Vulkan PRX copies
were replaced with the exact known-good 0981 files before regenerating the PKG.
The final staged-file diff against 0981 is limited to `wow64native.dll`, its
two Unix PRX copies, and package metadata (`pkg.gp4`, `param.sfo`). The final
package is 445,644,800 bytes, SHA-256
`953411acce2b58231845e658b5904556f861d90bb922349f8b95e48b8e21726d`.
GoldHEN FTP confirmed the complete direct upload (`226`) at
`/data/pkg/oblivion-callids-0982-reloc.pkg`. The existing klog, UDP, and ps5logd
listeners remain running; do not restart them.

For the next measurement, install 0982 over GLDS10056 and launch Oblivion with
no controller input during startup. Watch from `Oblivion.exe` start through the
first full-size 1920x1080 output; that is the comparison cutoff, about 12
minutes in prior valid runs. Retrieve the log immediately after that milestone
or if the app closes. Compare the `PW_NATIVE_CALL_IDS` lines for the busiest
thread/TEB over successive samples, alongside `PW_NATIVE_PROFILE` transition
rates and the existing process I/O counters. Do not wait through save loading
or gameplay for this startup experiment. The diagnostic package is intended
to name the hot dispatch family; it does not itself change scheduling,
filesystem behavior, or graphics.


#### Correction: 0982 companion rebuilt with the PS4 Wine loader adapter (2026-10-19)

The first uploaded 0982 package used a manually wrapped `wow64native.prx` and
was invalid for this port. The app crashed about 15 seconds after start, before
Oblivion entered its long-load phase. At native backend initialization,
`PW_NATIVE_PROVIDER` reported the signal bridge, but the companion logged
`PW_NATIVE_STAGE process_init query=0` / `missing-query`, returned
`STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED`, and entered the existing signal-fault termination
loop. No `PW_NATIVE_CALL_IDS` sample was emitted. This run provides no evidence
about startup latency. The direct coredump retrieval for
`/user/data/sce_coredumps/GLDS10056_1792444528/GLDS10056_1792444528.sorbisdmp`
returned FTP `550 File unavailable`; the klog shows the crash-reporter cleanup
path. Do not count this as a captured dump.

Cause found in the build path: the manual companion was not linked with the
PS4 `pw_wine_dl_libc.o` adapter that provides Wine's `dlsym` behavior. The
established `scripts/prx-ps4.sh` stage builds `wow64native` with that adapter.

#### 2026-10-19: 0982 native-call sample and slow-start result

The corrected 0982 package passed native startup and reached full-size output.
The process/profile began at 14:21:09.517 PDT, DXVK reported six compiler
threads at 14:23:08.302, and the first 1920x1080 output was reported at
14:32:20.310: 11m10.8s from profile start and 9m12.0s from the DXVK marker.
This is about 48 seconds faster than the prior ~10-minute compiler-to-output
measurements, but still an unacceptably slow startup. At first output the
runtime had only `frames_put=4`, `shown=1`; Vulkan present calls completed
successfully afterward, so this marks first full-size output, not confirmed
gameplay. At that point `cpu_ms=1,377,163`, `majflt=2375`, and `inblock=2675`.
By 14:34:26, `cpu_ms` had reached 1,654,613 while major faults stayed at
2,375 and `inblock` stayed at 4,288. Flexible memory remained available and
there were no dmem allocation failures. This remains CPU-active, with no
evidence that swapping or synchronous file reads explain the delay.

The 0982 `PW_NATIVE_CALL_IDS` sample identifies the hot profile thread as
Wine TID `002c` (TEB `7ffd6000`, matched against the same-run thread init
records). Every rolling sample in the slow phase contains Windows syscall
`0x4` and Unix-call ID `0x81300014`; `0x4` is `NtWaitForSingleObject` in this
build. The high 16 bits of the Unix-call word are the sampled module handle
(`0x8130`) and the low 16 bits are ordinal `0x14`; that module/ordinal has not
been conclusively mapped to a loaded module. However, the pattern `0x14` with
occasional `0x0a`/`0x0b` exactly matches entries in the PS4 build's
`wine/wineps5/unix.c` audio table: `ps5_get_current_padding`,
`ps5_get_render_buffer`, and `ps5_release_render_buffer`. This makes audio
polling the strongest current lead; confirm that handle `0x8130` resolves to
`wineps5` before treating it as proven. In the same run, TID `002c` also
timed out waiting for `loader_section`, blocked by TID `0024`, at 14:22:42.
The 0980
server trace already showed TID `002c` repeatedly waiting on event handle
`0100`, with immediate `signaled=1` completion, so the repeated wait itself
is not a slow kernel wait. Together, the measurements point to a hot
user-mode loop around an immediately satisfied wait and likely audio polling,
but do not establish whether Wine's event handling, audio backend, or the
game's polling behavior causes it.

Next, resolve Unix handle `0x8130` against the exact loaded module table and
instrument only the audio event association and bounded current-padding call
rate/state (stream, event handle, held frames, period). Correlate that with
the already captured wait result before considering any audio behavior
change. Do not repeat generic wait/file-I/O logging or the GPL A/B.
The current console run has already passed the startup measurement cutoff;
use the captured logs rather than spending another launch on the same
diagnostic unless a specific validation test is identified.

For that targeted validation, package 0983 adds bounded instrumentation to
`wine/wineps5/unix.c`: it logs the stream/event association once at
`SetEventHandle`, then reports `GetCurrentPadding` state every 262,144 calls
(plus the first call). It does not alter audio or wait behavior. The exact
source diff is recorded in
`src/wine-platform-patches-ps4/0003-diagnose-oblivion-audio-polling.patch`.
The rebuilt `wineps5.prx` in the package contains both `PW_AUDIO_EVENT` and
`PW_AUDIO_PADDING` markers and is byte-identical to
`prospero-win/.deps/wine-ps5/prx/sce_module/wineps5.prx`.

### Oblivion startup: DirectSound event-wait A/B (2026-10-19)

The 0983 audio diagnostic package was installed and launched on the console in
this session. The console entered the relocatable Wine launcher at 14:47:22 PDT
and loaded Oblivion at 14:47:47. `PW_AUDIO_EVENT` then reported stream
`0x88002a020`, event handle `0x100`, flags `0x800c0000`, `period_frames=480`,
and `period=100000` (10 ms).

This run separates initial file paging from the persistent stall. Major faults
jumped during startup to 2,375 by 14:49:11, then stayed at 2,375 through at
least 14:55:03. `inblock` rose slowly from about 1,300 to 2,203 after the
initial burst. This does not look like sustained swapping or ongoing bulk file
reads.

The audio path is now the strongest measured lead. `GetCurrentPadding` reached
5,242,880 calls at 14:55:06; later samples show 262,144 calls every ~16.9 s
(about 15,500 calls/s), with `held=2208`, `period_frames=480`, and `playing=1`
unchanged. The native-profile thread is TEB `7ffd6000`, Wine TID `002c`, the
same thread that previously waited on event `0x100`. By 14:55:10 it had
10,747,905 host/guest calls, split between 5,440,744 Unix calls and 5,307,161
syscall calls. At that point the runtime still reported only three frames
submitted, last size 326x272, while the major-fault count had long since
stabilized. This is far beyond the expected rate for DirectSound's event wait
path.

Source review found the exact caller: Wine DirectSound creates an auto-reset
event (`CreateEventW(0, 0, 0, 0)`), asks WASAPI for
`AUDCLNT_STREAMFLAGS_EVENTCALLBACK`, passes that event to `SetEventHandle`, and
its mixer thread runs `WaitForSingleObject(sleepev, sleeptime)` before calling
`DSOUND_PerformMix` / `GetCurrentPadding`. Without event callbacks, the existing
mixer uses its timed-wait fallback (5 ms or 2.5 times the stream period). The
PS4 audio backend signals the configured event from its 256-frame mixer at the
480-frame period boundary.

Package 0984 is a controlled A/B: the i386 and x86_64 Wine `dsound.dll` files
were rebuilt with DirectSound event-callback initialization and
`SetEventHandle` disabled, allowing the existing timed-wait fallback to drive
the mixer. This is diagnostic, not a proposed final audio change. If the
startup/frame rate improves, the event-wait path is implicated; if it does
not, investigate a different native-thread loop. The test may alter audio
smoothness. All other 1,785 staged payload files are byte-identical to 0983
except the title SFO; both DSOUND DLLs are the only runtime payload changes.
The same GLDS10056 identity, eboot, and relocatable Wine PRX are retained.

Package:
`pkgout/oblivion-dsound-timer-ab-0984-reloc/IV0000-GLDS10056_00-GLDS100560000000.pkg`

Uploaded through `scripts/ftp-put.py` with no FTP login to
`/data/pkg/oblivion-dsound-timer-ab-0984-reloc.pkg`; GoldHEN returned `226`.
Do not install until the current 0983 run is closed; both packages share
GLDS10056. The klog, UDP, and ps5log listeners remain running.

The experiment patch is recorded at
`src/wine-platform-patches-ps4/0004-dsound-timed-wait-ab.patch`; the patched
Wine source is in the ignored `prospero-win/.deps/wine-host/source` tree, and
the two stripped DLLs are preserved under `~/codex_scratch/dsound-timer-ab/`.

#### 0986 mixer wait timing and 0987 kqueue A/B (2026-10-19)

0986 (`oblivion-mixer-wait-0986-reloc`) added bounded timing around
`DSOUND_mixthread`'s `WaitForSingleObject(dev->sleepev, dev->sleeptime)`.
During this run the user measured about 1:27 from launch to leaving the dots,
then 13 seconds on the small window. The first small-window frame appeared in
the log at 15:44:47, about 1:44 after the game process started at 15:43:03.
The direct-sound mixer diagnostics began after that frame, so this mixer loop
does not explain the initial dots-to-window delay.

The mixer wait itself is demonstrably too short: samples show
`requested_ms=40`, `timeout=4096`, `signaled=0`, and only 253–266 ms elapsed
for each batch of 4,096 waits. This is about 15,000 mixer iterations per second
instead of roughly 25, and matches the high `GetCurrentPadding` call rate from
0985. It identifies a timed-wait defect on the native path, though not yet its
effect on later gameplay performance.

The PS4 cross configuration defines both epoll and kqueue support, and Wine's
`server/fd.c` selects epoll first. A/B package 0987 therefore changes only the
Wine server wait backend on Orbis to use its existing kqueue path. The Orbis
headers lacked `NOTE_LOWAT`, so the patch defines that flag as zero only when
building for Orbis; the read/write filters retain their default low-water
behavior. The candidate is tracked at
`src/wine-patches-ps4/0960-server-use-kqueue-on-orbis.patch`. The PS4 wineserver
PRX chain rebuilt successfully after directly compiling `server/fd.o`; the
standalone `server/wineserver` link is not a valid PS4 target because the PS4
shim symbols are supplied by the PRX link script.

window to full-size. So the direct-server A/B did not materially improve either
startup stage (the second interval differs by about two seconds).

The process remained alive, with `majflt=2375` flat after 17:42:12 and no dmem
allocation failures; block input increased from 1,239 to 4,327 by 17:55, while
flexible free memory remained 137 MiB and dmem was 316 MiB. This does not
support ongoing major-fault paging as the cause of the long post-window wait.
Successful Vulkan presents began at 17:52:41; by 17:54:41–17:55:54 the
checkpoint count advanced by 120 about every two seconds (approximately 60
presents/s). Thus graphics eventually progressed despite the wrapper reporting
only four submitted frames. At 17:55:05 the native profile had 26,476,545 host
transitions and 13,093,976 syscalls; the DirectSound padding counter was
13,107,200. The 0990 stage lacked the `PW_DSOUND_WAIT`-instrumented DSOUND
DLL, so this run cannot compare mixer wait timing against 0989. The server-direct
toggle did not shorten the measured launch-to-window/menu progression.

The active listeners and logs remain in place. Do not repeat the server-direct
A/B. Next analyze the 0990 log around the transition from the small window to
the 1080p output and correlate the audio thread's native transition rate with
actual Vulkan presents; prioritize a targeted wait/audio change only if those
records identify the interval responsible.
