#### 2026-10-20: startup-regression review and translator control

The user selected `cpu=translator` in the Oblivion profile to check whether the
current startup slowdown is specific to native WoW64. The launch log confirmed
`cpu=translator prefix_cpu=-2`; it reached its first 326x272 frame after about
97 seconds, then remained at `frames_put=4`, `last=326x272` for over six
minutes. This is a valid shared-runtime regression signal, though it is not yet
a controlled comparison with the earlier ORBW00053 package and pre-update
prefix.

The current run is not showing evidence of swapping: dmem allocation failures
remain zero, flexible memory is about 230 MiB free, major faults have stayed
flat at 4,623 since early startup, and block input has risen only gradually.
The current profile also has `show_fps=0` and reduced Wine debug logging.

The strongest measured shared-path anomaly is DirectSound's mixer wait loop:
each 4,096 waits request 40 ms, but all return as timeouts in about 181–185 ms
total (roughly 22,000 waits/s). The count has passed five million while the
display remains at the small launch frame. This can consume a CPU core and is
present with the translator selected, so native guest execution is not required
to produce it. It is a concrete lead, not yet proof that it alone explains the
full load delay.

The current 0991 stage compared with 0981 differs in runtime binaries
`relocated_wine_app.prx`, `wineps5.prx`, `wineserver.prx`, `wow64native.prx`,
and `libvulkan.prx`, plus the instrumented `dsound.dll` and `mmdevapi.dll` in
both architectures and package metadata. Most ordinary Wine PE DLLs are
identical. 0991 is based on 0990 and has `server_direct=0`; 0981 predates that
override. The DirectSound instrumentation is sampled once per 4,096 waits and
cannot plausibly account for the multi-minute delay by itself.

Changes since the Oct 16 working translator run divide into:

- Native-only WoW64 bridge, TEB/signal setup, and native profiling changes:
  not a sufficient explanation, because the present run explicitly selected
  the translator.
- Shared PS4 signal/VM changes (SIGSYS/single-step handling and executable
  page protection), plus Oblivion's bounded RX-retry workaround: still in the
  binary, but no evidence yet that these paths are hot during this run.
- Filesystem and wait changes: PS4 directory enumeration, kqueue selection,
  server wait behavior, and `server_direct=0` can affect translator mode and
  remain candidates. The previous kqueue A/B did not materially change the
  measured startup, and the 0990 native server-direct A/B did not materially
  change timing; neither result fully isolates the current translator path.
- Console/prefix state also changed after the system update and data restore,
  so the Oct 16 run is not a same-state baseline. The replacement HDD has
  since been exercised successfully, and current counters do not support
  ongoing storage paging as the explanation for the present stall.

Do not attribute this to native CPU support or memory swapping. Once the user
closes this run, the next useful comparison is a same-prefix translator A/B
that restores the prior server mode while changing no other runtime binaries;
compare the 40 ms wait timing, time to first 326x272 wrapper frame, the first
1920x1080 DXVK swapchain/present, and the user's observed menu/game milestone.
The wrapper `frames_put` counter does not count ordinary DXVK presents. Keep
listeners running and do not perform FTP directory probes.

affect a run explicitly configured for the translator. The v0.2.0 release also
changed shared paths used by translated games, including Vulkan batching and
shader compilation/cache behavior, thread scheduling, shared input/clock
options, diagnostics, and the CPU-count report. It also added fixes for fixed
reservations, memory accounting, and other Wine behavior. See the upstream
v0.2.0 release notes. If 10060 is still slow, it is not evidence that v0.2.0
is harmless; it only excludes the omitted native payload as the cause. The
next useful build must restore a pre-v0.2.0 runtime baseline while retaining
the minimum PS4 launch, relocation, filesystem, input, and packaging changes,
or use same-runtime A/B switches for individual shared changes. Do not call a
plain v0.1.1 package a valid control unless those PS4 requirements are ported
and verified.

v0.2.0 release was tagged on 2026-10-10 and contains further shared runtime
changes after #432. A build from `73675f7` with `cpu=translator`, relocation,
and the required PS4 portability fixes would test those post-#432 changes; a
build from v0.1.1 would test the whole v0.2.0 delta and would need more PS4
porting. Keep these as distinct possible controls rather than calling either
one “the pre-native build.”

The slow behavior is unchanged on the latest run. The newly recovered profiler
snapshot at 13:29:24 reported `ticks=1,663,673`, `unattributed=1,424,894`;
the dominant sample PCs remain the same. Disassembly of the saved libkernel
text confirms these are syscall return stubs: `+0x12fc` is syscall 331
(`sched_yield`), `+0x0cdc` is 117 (`select`), `+0x1ddc` is 601 (Sony's
`sys_mdbg_service`), and `+0x2a7c` is 340 (`sigprocmask2`). The PS4 syscall
table defines 331 and 340 in [mast1c0re's PS syscall header](https://github.com/McCaulay/mast1c0re/blob/master/sdk/include/ps/syscall/table.hpp);
the Sony custom syscall table names 601 in the [PS4 Developer wiki](https://www.psdevwiki.com/ps4/Syscalls).
The relative counts put `sched_yield` first, followed by `select`; this is a
poll/wait/synchronization lead, not evidence of file reads or memory pressure.
The signal profiler samples at 1 kHz, so the next step is to measure call sites
from the saved RSP when a sample lands inside libkernel. That distinguishes a
Wine module's hot loop from the syscall wrapper itself.

#### 10069 eboot failure and corrected 10070 package (2026-10-20)

The user installed and launched GLDS10069 twice. Both runs failed before Wine
started: `eboot.bin` printed the executable-memory probe and only the first two
of four libkernel nearest-export results, then raised `SIGILL` at RIP
`0x400a83` (`reason: privileged instruction fault`). No
`wowprospero syscall_caller:` output is expected from these runs. The crash
reporter deleted the coredump after reporting it; the reconnecting klog has the
full fault context.

The mistake was the package base: 10069 had been repackaged from
`oblivion-win32u-prx-ab-10068-profilemap/pkg-stage`, whose diagnostic eboot

10069 base) and `a467cf819fab490a482afeb45d6ea7b10e7ca3eb8ed4ae664910a8051b0b0574`
(known 10068 package base). This is an eboot diagnostic failure, not evidence
against the syscall-caller profiler.

#### Startup latency is the gate (2026-10-20)

The user clarified that long gameplay runs are not useful until startup latency
is fixed. Do not wait through gameplay/load-save cycles for performance results.
For this regression, use the first small 326x272 Oblivion launch-window frame
as the bounded milestone; the user explicitly said this is sufficient and we do
not need to wait for the full-size/gameplay frame.

The GLDS10070 launch log shows `cpu=translator`. In its first ~113 seconds,
process CPU time rose to about 130 seconds, major faults rose from 250 to
4,490, and block input rose from 80 to 1,327. Memory remained healthy (about
236 MiB flexible memory free, no dmem allocation failures). This rules out
obvious swap pressure, but the counters show both substantial CPU work and
some page-in activity; they do not yet assign the delay to one subsystem.
The tiny 326x272 wrapper frame is not the startup completion marker. It is
followed by continued slow work, so do not call the process hung based only on
the stable wrapper frame.

**10073 result:** The user confirmed this launch felt faster. The app log has
`launcher chose=oblivion-gog` at 14:26:16.301, Wine server connected at
14:26:17.849, and the first `frames_put=1`, `last=326x272` at 14:26:38.489:
22.2 seconds from profile selection (20.6 seconds after server connect), versus
82.0 seconds on 10072. By 14:26:40 the first full-size 1920x1080 frame had
also been submitted. The klog's last RX migration/protection entries are from
14:25:20, before this launch; none occurred during the 10073 run. This is a
large A/B improvement and strongly implicates the actually loaded NTDLL's RX
migration/protection path in the startup regression, even on `cpu=translator`.
It does not yet prove that RX handling explains all remaining startup time:
10073 still took about 22 seconds to show its first small frame, and the old
NTDLL changes other behavior as well. Preserve 10073 as the fast control and
bisect the loaded NTDLL changes between the 10062 and 10072 versions (or
selectively disable only the RX migration/verification feature) while keeping
the rest of the payload and profile fixed. Do not wait through gameplay for
this startup comparison. Existing listeners remain running.

**10074 result:** This no-migration variant was slower, as the user observed.
The launcher chose `oblivion-gog` at 14:35:09.929; the first telemetry report
with `frames_put=3`, `last=326x272` arrived at 14:36:46.694 (about 97 seconds
from selection), versus 22 seconds on 10073. The klog shows hundreds of
`exec-protect-verify` results where `sceKernelMprotect` returned success but
the queried protection was only `0x1` or `0x3` (no `PROT_EXEC`); migration was
disabled (`migrate=-2`). The process eventually reached the small frame, but
the executable mappings were not actually repaired. So simply removing the
fallback is not a viable optimization. This is evidence that the memory path
matters, but 10074 does not show that the migration itself is the dominant
cost: verification and synchronous logging remained enabled, and missing RX
could cause retries/faults elsewhere.

The user emphasized that the objective is scalable range-level memory
handling, not tuning a repair for each page. Treat per-page builds only as
bounded diagnostics. `pw_wine_dmem_replace()` and `pw_wine_dmem_adopt()` accept
aligned multi-page ranges, and `pw_wine_dmem_backed()` reports backing across a
range, so investigate coalescing contiguous direct-backed executable spans;
preserve holes, mixed backing, failure rollback, and the existing image-size
bound. Do not ship a per-page repair as the final approach.

**10076 first hardware run crashed during Wine startup.** The live klog shows
the RX range migration completing for the guest image and several Wine
modules at 14:56:32.686–.691, then a fatal `SIGILL` on thread `orbis` at
14:56:32.691. The kernel described it as a user read page fault at
`0x000000100010b5e8`; RIP was `0x00000008000009fe` in `libkernel.sprx`, with
RSP equal to the fault address. The app logger had reached
`server_init_process_done` but disconnected before normal startup telemetry.
This is a real early crash, but the dump was automatically deleted by Crash
Reporter at 14:56:40, and the klog has no usable backtrace (`copyin: orbis has
nonsleeping lock`). Do not claim yet that range migration caused it: 10072
also had a later fatal signal, but that was an unrelated GPU submit-done
timeout during suspension. The direct comparison is **10075**, which retains
10072's page-at-a-time migration and suppresses only successful per-page

for a repeat run at the user's request. It is built as
